But yeah, no, I'm definitely not trying to be skinny or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of eating just doesn't appeal to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. It's just... most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, you know? Like the day is already ruined before I even put on my badge. And speaking of my badge, the clip on it broke last week, so I've been having to carry it in my pocket. It's such a hassle because the card readers at the hospital are so finicky. You have to hold it exactly right or it just flashes red at you. The one by the service elevator is the worst. I swear, I stood there for five minutes the other day just swiping and swiping. This guy from maintenance—Dave, I think his name is, tall guy, always wears a baseball cap—he saw me struggling and told me you have to kind of tap it on the corner, not flat against the scanner. Which, I mean, why even have a flat scanner if you're supposed to tap the corner? It makes no sense. But anyway, yeah, I just feel really weighed down lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I just... I don't really care about doing much of anything anymore. I used to love going for runs in Van Cortlandt Park, you know? It's really beautiful there, especially in the fall when the leaves change. I'd take my camera—this old Nikon DSLR I bought off Craigslist years ago. The guy I bought it from was actually a wedding photographer who was upgrading all his gear. He threw in a couple of extra lenses, which was really nice of him. He said his wife was making him clean out the garage because they were expecting a baby. I wonder how that kid is doing now... probably in middle school by now. \n\nBut yeah, I'd take photos, or I'd meet up with some buddies to play chess. We had this whole weekend routine. Now, though, I just tell myself there's no point. I don't even bother taking the camera out of its bag. I just end up sitting on my couch, scrolling on my phone or staring at my budget spreadsheet for hours. And let me tell you, looking at a budget spreadsheet right now is just depressing. The price of groceries has gone up so much. A carton of eggs is ridiculous. I went to the C-Town on the corner the other day and they wanted almost six dollars for a dozen. Six dollars! The cashier, Maria, she said people complain to her all day about it like she's the one setting the prices. Poor woman. She works so hard, always has a smile on her face, but you can tell she's exhausted. Anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just don't have the energy or the interest for any of my old hobbies lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I mean, I haven't made a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I definitely catch myself wishing I just wouldn't wake up in the morning. Like, maybe it would just be easier if I disappeared, you know? It's just so exhausting to keep going. \n\nSpeaking of exhausting, the alarm clock I have is just the absolute worst. It's one of those old digital ones with the red numbers, and the buzzer is so loud and abrasive. I bought it at a thrift store years ago because I thought it looked retro, but honestly, it just gives me a headache. The thrift store is down on 238th Street, run by this really sweet older lady named Mrs. Higgins. She always has a bowl of those strawberry candies with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries. You know the ones? My grandma used to keep those in her purse. She’d give me one whenever we had to sit through a long sermon at church. The pews there were so uncomfortable, solid oak, and they’d just dig into your back. But yeah, sometimes I just lie there listening to that awful alarm buzzing and think... it would be so much easier to just not have to get up and face it all.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after something particularly stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess you could trace it back to when the clinic completely overhauled the electronic medical records system and changed up the whole workflow. That messed with my hours pretty badly. It's been almost two years since that happened, if you can believe it. Two years. \n\nAnd man, that new EMR system... it's a nightmare. They brought in these consultants, right? These guys in sharp suits who probably haven't stepped foot in a clinic since they got their flu shots. They kept talking about \"synergy\" and \"optimizing patient throughput.\" One of them, I think his name was Chad, he kept using the word \"bandwidth\" to talk about human beings. Like we're routers or something. Anyway, they switched us from this older system—which, sure, was a little clunky, but we all knew how to use it—to this shiny new one that crashes if you look at it wrong. \n\nI remember the first week we went live with it, the whole network went down for three hours. Three hours! We had patients sitting in the waiting room getting more and more agitated. Mrs. Gable, she's this sweet lady who comes in for her blood pressure checks, she brought us a tin of homemade snickerdoodles that day. She always brings cookies. Her husband used to be a baker before he passed away. He made the best pumpernickel bread in the Bronx. But yeah, we were all just sitting there eating snickerdoodles while the IT guys ran around sweating through their polo shirts. So, yeah, the stress from all that change and my hours getting shifted around... that's when this whole heavy feeling really started settling in, about two years ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this down most days for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Well, not the whole two years, actually. The stress from work started then, but the really steady part... like the heavy depression and just completely losing interest in everything, that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. \n\nA year and four months... so that would put it right around October, I guess? Yeah, October. That's always when we do the big flu shot push at the clinic. Man, those flu clinics are a complete madhouse. We have these cheap little folding tables we set up in the hallway to check people in, and they are so wobbly. I tried to fix one with a folded-up piece of cardboard under the leg, but it didn't really do much. We had this one guy come in for his shot around then—big guy, maybe six-foot-four, covered in motorcycle tattoos—and he was absolutely terrified of needles. Passed out right on the linoleum floor before the nurse even took the cap off the syringe. Sounded like a sack of potatoes hitting the ground. We had to use smelling salts to wake him up. Have you ever smelled those things? It's like getting punched in the brain with ammonia. My uncle Arthur used to keep some in his gym bag back in the 80s when he was into powerlifting. He ended up tearing his rotator cuff and never lifted again, though. Just started selling life insurance out in Jersey instead. He drove this really ugly brown Buick LeSabre... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, the timeline. Right. So, yeah, it hasn't been two years of feeling *this* down. The constant depression, that's just been the last year and four months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. I just... I can't seem to hold onto a thought lately. Like, I'll be trying to read a chart or even just watch a movie, and my brain just sort of floats away. It's frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of movies, I tried to watch that new superhero one last weekend. The one with the guy who shrinks? My nephew was telling me all about it. He's twelve now, can you believe it? Twelve. He just started middle school and he's playing the trombone. God, that instrument is loud. My sister, she makes him practice in the garage, but you can still hear it from the street. It sounds like a dying elephant sometimes. I remember when I was in middle school, I tried to play the clarinet. I was terrible at it. I could never get the reed wet enough, or maybe I was biting down too hard, I don't know. It always squeaked. We had this band director, Mr. Gable—not related to the snickerdoodle lady, different Gable—and he had this combover that would flap up whenever he got really into conducting. We used to place bets on how many times it would flip over during a song. I won five bucks once during the spring concert... \n\nBut yeah, concentrating. It's just... it's hard to stay focused on anything for very long right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's really not going well. My sleep is all broken up. I just lie there at night, and my brain is like a tape recorder on loop, just replaying everything from work. Every little interaction, every chart I might have filed wrong. And I'm always listening for noises, you know? Like every creak in the house. \n\nMy house is pretty old, actually. Built in the 1920s. The plumbing is a nightmare. I had a plumber come out last month—this guy named Sal. He showed up in this van that looked like it hadn't been washed since the Clinton administration. He had this massive wrench, looked like something out of a cartoon. And he charged me an arm and a leg just to tell me the pipes are galvanized and rusting from the inside out. He said I need a whole house repipe. Can you imagine? Ripping out all the walls. The dust alone would kill me. I have terrible allergies. Ragweed just destroys me in the fall. I have to buy those expensive air purifiers with the HEPA filters. I got one on sale at Target, though. They have a really good clearance section if you know where to look. I found a waffle iron there once for like ten bucks. It makes those really thick Belgian waffles... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I lie there listening to the pipes and worrying about work, and then I finally drift off, but I always wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning. And that's it. I just can't settle back down before my alarm goes off. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my coworkers have definitely noticed. It's embarrassing. At work, I either pace around the supply area checking the exact same drawers over and over, or I move so slowly that drawing up labels and answering a patient takes me twice as long as it should. \n\nThe labels are a whole thing, honestly. We switched to this new printer system back in January, and it's supposedly \"wireless\" but it loses connection to the network at least three times a shift. The IT guy, Kevin, he comes down to fix it, and he always smells like those cool ranch Doritos. Always. I don't know how he eats them so early in the morning. I used to love Doritos when I was a kid, but now they just give me terrible heartburn. I have to take Tums like they're candy if I eat anything spicy. My doctor—my primary care guy, Dr. Evans—he told me I need to cut back on acidic foods. He gave me this whole pamphlet with a picture of a sad-looking stomach on the front. It was printed on this really glossy paper. I hate glossy paper, it always leaves fingerprints when you touch it. My ex-wife used to buy these glossy fashion magazines, and they would just pile up on the coffee table... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the pacing or just moving like I'm underwater. People have definitely pointed it out to me at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energized or high, much more than your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I've never really felt like that. I mean, I'm usually pretty low-energy these days anyway. I definitely don't have periods where I'm bouncing off the walls or anything. \n\nActually, the last time I felt really energetic was probably back when I was running half-marathons. That was... gosh, almost ten years ago now. I used to run with this group on Saturday mornings. We'd meet up at the park by the river. There was this one guy in the group, Dave, who always wore these neon green shorts. Like, blindingly bright. He said it was for safety, so cars would see him, but we ran on a paved trail where there weren't any cars. I think he just liked the attention. He was always trying to sell us on these weird energy gels, too. They tasted like battery acid mixed with artificial cherry flavor. I tried one once and I swear my stomach was doing flips for three days. I had to call out of work. That was back when I was working at the old clinic on Elm Street, before they tore it down to build that strip mall. It's a shame, really, it was a nice brick building. Now it's just a mattress store and a frozen yogurt place. I don't even understand how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. How often do people really buy mattresses? You buy one, what, every ten years? And yet there's one on every corner...\n\nBut no, to answer your question, I've never had any kind of crazy high energy like you're talking about. Just the opposite, really.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it’s getting bad. I’ve started making these completely avoidable mistakes at work, and I constantly need my coworkers to repeat really simple requests. Like, they'll ask me to restock something and I just blank. My coworker Brenda, she had to tell me three times yesterday. Brenda has this weird habit of chewing ice all day. She brings in this massive insulated cup, one of those Yeti ones that weigh like five pounds, and just crunches on it. It’s terrible for your teeth. My dentist, Dr. Gable, he’s always lecturing me about enamel. He has this massive fish tank in his waiting room. Neon tetras, mostly. I always wanted a fish tank, but the maintenance is just too much. The filters get all slimy, and you have to balance the pH of the water... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, work is a struggle. And my own time isn't any better. I’ve been completely avoiding my regular chess nights, and I stopped going on my weekend photography walks. I just... I don’t trust myself to keep up with anyone or act normal right now. The photography walks were my favorite, too. We’d go down to the nature preserve. The light there in the late afternoon is incredible. I shoot on an old 35mm film camera, and finding 35mm film now is like finding treasure. You have to order it online, and half the time the packages get lost. The delivery drivers just leave things right on the edge of my porch where anyone could take them, or where the rain just ruins the cardboard. I really should get one of those doorbell cameras, but I don't like the idea of being recorded all the time... \n\nSo, yeah. I just stay home instead. It's just too hard to function out there with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I mean, I guess I feel a little tense sometimes, but not really anxious or on edge, no. Not like a panic thing. I just feel... heavy, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking non-stop lately, and that definitely sets my teeth on edge. It's this little terrier mix, looks like a wire brush with legs. They leave it out in the yard all day while they're at work, and it just yaps at every single leaf that blows by. I tried talking to them about it, the neighbors, I mean. They're this young couple, just moved in a few months ago. They drive this electric car, one of those Teslas with the flush door handles. I don't trust those door handles. What if the battery dies and you're trapped inside? I read an article about that once in waiting room at the Jiffy Lube. I always get my oil changed there because they give you a little sticker for your windshield. I like the reminder. I'm terrible at remembering maintenance stuff. Like my gutters, I haven't cleaned them out since last fall, and now they're probably full of maple seeds. Those little helicopter things? They get everywhere. When I was a kid, we used to stick them on our noses... \n\nBut no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling anxious myself. Just tired and slow.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The light there in the late afternoon is incredible. I shoot on an old 35mm film camera, and finding 35mm film now is like finding treasure. You have to order it online, and half the time the packages get lost. The delivery drivers just leave things right on the edge of my porch where anyone could take them, or where the rain just ruins the cardboard. I really should get one of those doorbell cameras, but I don't like the idea of being recorded all the time... \n\nSo, yeah. I just stay home instead. It's just too hard to function out there with everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I just feel... heavy, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking non-stop lately, and that definitely sets my teeth on edge. It's this little terrier mix, looks like a wire brush with legs. They leave it out in the yard all day while they're at work, and it just yaps at every single leaf that blows by. I tried talking to them about it, the neighbors, I mean. They're this young couple, just moved in a few months ago. They drive this electric car, one of those Teslas with the flush door handles. I don't trust those door handles. What if the battery dies and you're trapped inside? I read an article about that once in waiting room at the Jiffy Lube. I always get my oil changed there because they give you a little sticker for your windshield. I like the reminder. I'm terrible at remembering maintenance stuff. Like my gutters, I haven't cleaned them out since last fall, and now they're probably full of maple seeds. Those little helicopter things? They get everywhere. When I was a kid, we used to stick them on our noses... \n\nBut no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling anxious myself. Just tired and slow."
